### `bio-causal-genomics-colocalization-analysis` — harmonise() has no strand-complement-flip branch and no pitfall callout for it

- Skill: 96, Production Ready · [mrsonord2240/bioSkills@9da81ea](https://github.com/mrsonord2240/bioSkills/tree/9da81ea758e0b0d631f72199a4679211dc89f5d4/causal-genomics/colocalization-analysis) · [viewer](skills/bio-causal-genomics-colocalization-analysis/mrsonord2240-bioSkills@9da81ea/viewer.md)
- Observed in inputs: 7
- Problem: A SNP pair coded on opposite strands without being palindromic (e.g. A/G vs its complement T/C) is neither 'same' nor 'flip' under harmonise()'s literal definition and is silently dropped. None of the five 'Harmonisation pitfalls to watch for' bullets mention this case (they cover build mismatch and palindromic-at-high-MAF only).
- Root cause: harmonise() implements only an allele-order-swap check, not a strand-complement check, and the pitfalls list was not updated to reflect this scope limit when the function moved from usage-guide.md into SKILL.md this fix pass.
- Fix: Add a sixth pitfall bullet documenting that harmonise() does not resolve non-palindromic strand mismatches and that upstream strand-harmonization is required before calling it, or extend the function with a complement-check branch if silent SNP loss at scale is a concern.

### `bio-metabolomics-statistical-analysis` — Correlated-feature FDR collapse produced 1 false independent-signal compound this run

- Skill: 96, Production Ready · [mrsonord2240/bioSkills@6847328](https://github.com/mrsonord2240/bioSkills/tree/684732876d2781df75d90ba35c3e9949ff4f28b2/metabolomics/statistical-analysis) · [viewer](skills/bio-metabolomics-statistical-analysis/mrsonord2240-bioSkills@6847328/viewer.md)
- Observed in inputs: 5
- Problem: The compound-level BH-collapsed hit list included 1 compound with no planted true signal, versus 0 false compound hits in the pre-fix audit's synthetic run.
- Root cause: Expected sampling variation at n=25/group with 90 compounds and per-compound Bonferroni-within-block p-value collapsing; not a methodological error.
- Fix: No SKILL.md change needed; note in usage-guide.md that the compound-level correction still has a small non-zero false-discovery rate by construction (it is BH, not a zero-FDR guarantee) so users should not read '0 false hits' as a promise.

### `bio-pathway-gsea` — GSVA's kcdf parameter choice remains undocumented

- Skill: 97, Production Ready · [mrsonord2240/bioSkills@6847328](https://github.com/mrsonord2240/bioSkills/tree/684732876d2781df75d90ba35c3e9949ff4f28b2/pathway-analysis/gsea) · [viewer](skills/bio-pathway-gsea/mrsonord2240-bioSkills@6847328/viewer.md)
- Observed in inputs: 4
- Problem: gsva()/ssgsea() silently choose kcdf='auto' on the caller's behalf; this is the one parameter that changes GSVA/ssGSEA results between logCPM and raw-count input, and it is still never mentioned in SKILL.md or usage-guide.md. This finding was in the pre-fix audit's Input 4 assertions but was never promoted to a recommendation, so it was outside the fixer's 7-item scope and remains open.
- Root cause: The Per-Sample Scores section documents the parameter-object API change but not the kernel/CDF choice GSVA makes internally.
- Fix: Add one line noting that gsva()/ssgseaParam() choose kcdf='auto' by default, that this should be set explicitly (kcdf='Gaussian' for logCPM/microarray-like continuous data, 'Poisson' for raw counts), and that the choice should be logged alongside the other reporting requirements in 'The Single Most Important Modern Insight'.

### `bio-pathway-gsea` — fgsea's own 'ties in the preranked stats' warning is never documented

- Skill: 97, Production Ready · [mrsonord2240/bioSkills@6847328](https://github.com/mrsonord2240/bioSkills/tree/684732876d2781df75d90ba35c3e9949ff4f28b2/pathway-analysis/gsea) · [viewer](skills/bio-pathway-gsea/mrsonord2240-bioSkills@6847328/viewer.md)
- Observed in inputs: 1, 2, 3, 6, 7
- Problem: The 'There are ties in the preranked stats (X% of the list)' warning fired in 5 of 7 inputs in this re-audit (and was also noted firing in the pre-fix audit's runs), yet no Per-Method Failure Mode or Common Errors row mentions it. An agent following the Skill has no guidance on when this warning matters (e.g. many ties from a coarse or duplicated ranking metric) versus when it is cosmetic (a handful of ties from floating-point coincidence, as in this report's runs).
- Root cause: The failure-mode catalogue was built around clusterProfiler's own thrown errors and the three targeted P1 defects; fgsea's own runtime warnings were not separately audited.
- Fix: Add a Common Errors row for the ties warning: state that a high tie percentage usually indicates a low-precision or degenerate ranking metric (e.g. bare integer ranks, or a metric with many exact duplicates) and should prompt re-checking the ranking statistic, while a low percentage (as in a continuous Wald-stat or t-stat ranking) is benign.
